But I have never binged or made myself throw up; I just feel better keeping my stomach as empty as possible.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight, or has your weight dropped below what is expected for your age and height?\n\nPatient: My doctor and my sisters keep saying I'm getting too thin, and I have noticed my work uniforms hang off me and my wedding ring spins on my finger. But I really don't think it is that bad. I still have this little pouch on my belly that I feel like I need to lose before I can stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your weight and body shape are much too large even when other people say you are underweight, or do you think your weight is actually normal?\n\nPatient: Even though my doctor and sisters say I'm underweight, I just don't see it that way at all. When I look in the mirror, I still see a little pouch on my stomach that makes me feel too big, so I feel like I need to lose a bit more before I can stop. I usually just shrug off their worries because of that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any changes in your menstrual periods, such as becoming irregular or stopping altogether?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm 64 years old and went through menopause quite a few years ago. Because of that, I haven't had a menstrual period in a very long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a lot of weight recently, or do you know if your weight is now below what is healthy for your height and age?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, I have lost quite a bit of weight; my work uniforms are hanging off me now and my wedding ring just spins on my finger. My primary doctor and my sisters keep telling me I'm too thin, but I usually just shrug their concerns off. When I look in the mirror, I still see a little pouch on my stomach and feel like I need to lose a bit more.

    "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in?\n\nPatient: Well... lately all I can think about is not getting bigger as I get older, and I judge myself by what the scale says more than anything else. It has just become so hard to stop worrying about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been intentionally restricting how much you eat or trying to lose weight, and if so have you had any episodes of binge eating or vomiting to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have been cutting back a lot. Most days I just pick at a little yogurt or some crackers and avoid things like rice, pasteles, and oil because I'm terrified of getting bigger. But I have never binged or made myself throw up; I just feel better keeping my stomach as empty as possible.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight, or has your weight dropped below what is expected for your age and height?\n\nPatient: My doctor and my sisters keep saying I'm getting too thin, and I have noticed my work uniforms hang off me and my wedding ring spins on my finger. But I really don't think it is that bad. I still have this little pouch on my belly that I feel like I need to lose before I can stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your weight and body shape are much too large even when other people say you are underweight, or do you think your weight is actually normal?\n\nPatient: Even though my doctor and sisters say I'm underweight, I just don't see it that way at all. When I look in the mirror, I still see a little pouch on my stomach that makes me feel too big, so I feel like I need to lose a bit more before I can stop. I usually just shrug off their worries because of that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any changes in your menstrual periods, such as becoming irregular or stopping altogether?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm 64 years old and went through menopause quite a few years ago. Because of that, I haven't had a menstrual period in a very long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a lot of weight recently, or do you know if your weight is now below what is healthy for your height and age?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have lost quite a bit of weight; my work uniforms are hanging off me now and my wedding ring just spins on my finger. My primary doctor and my sisters keep telling me I'm too thin, but I usually just shrug their concerns off. When I look in the mirror, I still see a little pouch on my stomach and feel like I need to lose a bit more.\n\nDoctor: When you look at your body, do you feel that you are still too heavy even though others say you are underweight, or do you recognize that you are already too thin?\n\nPatient: Even though my doctor and sisters tell me I'm too thin and my clothes and wedding ring hang off me, I still feel too heavy. When I look at myself, I shrug off their comments because I still see a little pouch on my belly. I really feel like I should lose a bit more weight before I can stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe your current weight is too low and that you need to gain weight, or do you still feel you are too heavy and need to keep losing weight?"

But I have never binged or made myself throw up; I just feel better keeping my stomach as empty as possible.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight, or has your weight dropped below what is expected for your age and height?\n\nPatient: My doctor and my sisters keep saying I'm getting too thin, and I have noticed my work uniforms hang off me and my wedding ring spins on my finger. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others. Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.
